exec
/opt/homebrew/bin/zsh -lc 'cat /Users/nicholas/.agents/skills/code-review/SKILL.md' in /Users/nicholas/develop/browser-life-s1-surfaces-1102
 succeeded in 0ms:
---
name: code-review
description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes — Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
---

Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:

- **Standards** — does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
- **Spec** — does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?

Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.

The issue tracker should have been provided to you — run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.

## Process

### 1. Pin the fixed point

Whatever the user said is the fixed point — a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.

Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.

Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here — not inside two parallel sub-agents.

### 2. Identify the spec source

Look for the originating spec, in this order:

1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) — fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
2. A path the user passed as an argument.
3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".

### 3. Identify the standards sources

Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

On top of whatever the repo documents, the Standards axis always carries the **smell baseline** below — a fixed set of Fowler code smells (_Refactoring_, ch.3) that applies even when a repo documents nothing. Two rules bind it:

- **The repo overrides.** A documented repo standard always wins; where it endorses something the baseline would flag, suppress the smell.
- **Always a judgement call.** Each smell is a labelled heuristic ("possible Feature Envy"), never a hard violation — and, like any standard here, skip anything tooling already enforces.

Each smell reads *what it is* → *how to fix*; match it against the diff:

- **Mysterious Name** — a function, variable, or type whose name doesn't reveal what it does or holds. → rename it; if no honest name comes, the design's murky.
- **Duplicated Code** — the same logic shape appears in more than one hunk or file in the change. → extract the shared shape, call it from both.
- **Feature Envy** — a method that reaches into another object's data more than its own. → move the method onto the data it envies.
- **Data Clumps** — the same few fields or params keep travelling together (a type wanting to be born). → bundle them into one type, pass that.
- **Primitive Obsession** — a primitive or string standing in for a domain concept that deserves its own type. → give the concept its own small type.
- **Repeated Switches** — the same `switch`/`if`-cascade on the same type recurs across the change. → replace with polymorphism, or one map both sites share.
- **Shotgun Surgery** — one logical change forces scattered edits across many files in the diff. → gather what changes together into one module.
- **Divergent Change** — one file or module is edited for several unrelated reasons. → split so each module changes for one reason.
- **Speculative Generality** — abstraction, parameters, or hooks added for needs the spec doesn't have. → delete it; inline back until a real need shows.
- **Message Chains** — long `a.b().c().d()` navigation the caller shouldn't depend on. → hide the walk behind one method on the first object.
- **Middle Man** — a class or function that mostly just delegates onward. → cut it, call the real target direct.
- **Refused Bequest** — a subclass or implementer that ignores or overrides most of what it inherits. → drop the inheritance, use composition.

### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel

Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.

**Standards sub-agent prompt** — include:

- The full diff command and commit list.
- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3, **plus the smell baseline from step 3** pasted in full — the sub-agent has no other access to it.
- The brief: "Report — per file/hunk where relevant — (a) every place the diff violates a documented standard: cite the standard (file + the rule); and (b) any baseline smell you spot: name it and quote the hunk. Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls — documented-standard breaches can be hard, but baseline smells are always judgement calls, and a documented repo standard overrides the baseline. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."

**Spec sub-agent prompt** — include:

- The diff command and commit list.
- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."

If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.

### 5. Aggregate

Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings — the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).

End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes — that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.

## Why two axes

A change can pass one axis and fail the other:

- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing → **Standards pass, Spec fail.**
- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions → **Spec pass, Standards fail.**

Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.

   101	Plus: the HTML twin rendered at 1280 and 375 px, light and dark, no horizontal scroll, no console error, no external request;
   102	every command in the page appears in its command table with a check; every surface has a verdict backed by a shot or log in
   103	`walkthrough.md`; Codex review (Sol 6.1, xhigh, read-only) addressed in `REVIEW.md`.
   104	
   105	## 7. How to try it (what the finished page tells a person)
   106	
   107	```bash
   108	pnpm install
   109	pnpm dev                       # lab, site, /selftest.html, /playground/, /particles/ on :5173
   110	bin/dev                        # lab + coordinator (:5173, :4000); LAB_PORT / PORT to move them
   111	bin/garden-dev                 # whole local garden at /garden/ (GARDEN_FOOD=1 for food)
   112	deno run -A --no-lock tools/island.ts --coordinator http://localhost:4000
   113	deno run -A --no-lock tools/run.ts ...     # exact short form fixed by the walk
   114	deno run -A --no-lock tools/lineage.ts ... # on that run
   115	```
   116	The printed commands are the port-qualified forms actually run (5301/6301), each marked with the default-port form (5173/4000) as a documented translation that was read, not run, so the page never claims to have run what it did not.
   117	URLs: `/lab/?world=breeder&seed=1`, `/lab/?world=gradient-m3&seed=1`, `/worlds/`, `/garden/`, `http://localhost:4000/index.html`.
   118	
   119	
   120	## 8. Non-goals and limits
   121	
   122	No physics, `RULE_VERSION`, `defaultConfig()` or preregistration change. No deploy, no paid or model call, no AWS, no
   123	`tools/bench.ts approve`, no PRs #60/#69/#130, no I01, no gene-v2 direction, no I03/I06 files. Not a redesign: a surface that
   124	is broken for a large reason is documented, not rebuilt. Status is reported on four separate axes per surface: implemented, locally verified (verdict), data available, deployed/externally verified. Live-host-only things (HTTPS, real phones) are 'not externally verified', not 'not built'.
   125	Waits-on-you items are copied from merged PRs and D-numbers with their defaults, not invented. If the MCP browser has no WebGPU
   126	the lab verdicts rest on Playwright Chrome and say so. One PR; no commits until the stage that allows it.
     1	// The 2 November 2026 prototype status (docs/prototypes-2026-11-02.md and its HTML twin) and the walk-through it was written
     2	// from (docs/prototypes-2026-11-02/). CPU only: reads files, starts nothing. What is checked: the seven surface sections in
     3	// order with their four labels; the twins carry the same headings, commands, table cells and link targets; every local link and
     4	// screenshot resolves; the screenshot set (PNG only, 8 to 16, each under 300 KB, header width 1280 or 375, listed in
     5	// shots.json and cited by both twins); every inventory row of the walk has a verdict, a deciding assertion and evidence that
     6	// exists, and the frozen inventory covers every Vite input and playground destination; the 18 October pages differ from
     7	// their original blobs by one pointer line only; every PR cited as [#N] has a link definition.
     8	import { createHash } from "node:crypto";
     9	import { existsSync, readdirSync, readFileSync, statSync } from "node:fs";
    10	import { join, resolve } from "node:path";
    11	import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
    12	import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
    13	
    14	const REPO = fileURLToPath(new URL("../..", import.meta.url));
    15	const DOCS = join(REPO, "docs");
    16	const SLICE = join(DOCS, "prototypes-2026-11-02");
    17	const MD = readFileSync(join(DOCS, "prototypes-2026-11-02.md"), "utf8");
    18	const HTML = readFileSync(join(DOCS, "prototypes-2026-11-02.html"), "utf8");
    19	const WALK = readFileSync(join(SLICE, "walkthrough.md"), "utf8");
    20	
    21	const SURFACES = ["Lab", "Public site", "Archipelago", "Shared garden", "AI naturalist", "Evolution game", "Research instruments"];
    22	const LABELS = ["What it is now.", "How to try it.", "Not built.", "Waits on you."];
    23	const VERDICTS = ["works", "partly", "broken"];
    24	
    25	// ---- parsing helpers --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    26	
    27	const decode = (s: string) => s.replace(/&lt;/g, "<").replace(/&gt;/g, ">").replace(/&quot;/g, '"').replace(/&#x27;|&#39;/g, "'").replace(/&amp;/g, "&");
    28	const norm = (s: string) => decode(s).replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim();
    29	
    30	/** The md source with fenced blocks cut out (returned separately). */
    31	function splitFences(md: string): { prose: string; fences: string[] } {
    32	  const fences: string[] = [];
    33	  const prose = md.replace(/^```[^\n]*\n([\s\S]*?)^```\s*$/gm, (_m, body: string) => { fences.push(body.replace(/\n$/, "")); return ""; });
    34	  return { prose, fences };
    35	}
    36	
    37	function linkDefs(md: string): Map<string, string> {
    38	  const defs = new Map<string, string>();
    39	  for (const m of md.matchAll(/^\[(#\d+)\]: (\S+)$/gm)) defs.set(m[1], m[2]);
    40	  return defs;
    41	}
    42	
    43	/** Plain text of a markdown fragment: markup removed so it compares with the HTML's text. */
    44	function mdText(s: string): string {
    45	  return norm(
    46	    s
    47	      .replace(/!\[([^\]]*)\]\(([^)]+)\)/g, "")
    48	      .replace(/\[([^\]]+)\]\(([^)]+)\)/g, "$1")
    49	      .replace(/<(https?:\/\/[^>]+)>/g, "$1")
    50	      .replace(/\\\|/g, "|")
    51	      .replace(/`/g, "")
    52	      .replace(/\*\*/g, ""),
    53	  );
    54	}
    55	
    56	interface Parsed { headings: string[]; fences: string[]; code: string[]; cells: string[]; links: string[]; images: string[] }
    57	
    58	function parseMd(md: string): Parsed {
    59	  const defs = linkDefs(md);
    60	  const { prose: withDefs, fences } = splitFences(md);
    61	  const prose = withDefs.replace(/^\[#\d+\]: \S+$/gm, "");
    62	  const headings = [...prose.matchAll(/^#{1,2} (.*)$/gm)].map((m) => mdText(m[1]));
    63	  const code = [...prose.matchAll(/`([^`]+)`/g)].map((m) => m[1].replace(/\\\|/g, "|"));
    64	  const cells: string[] = [];
    65	  for (const line of prose.split("\n")) {
    66	    if (!line.startsWith("|") || /^\|[\s|:-]+\|$/.test(line)) continue;
    67	    for (const c of line.trim().slice(1, -1).split(/(?<!\\)\|/)) cells.push(mdText(c));
    68	  }
    69	  const links: string[] = [];
    70	  const images: string[] = [];
    71	  for (const m of prose.matchAll(/(!?)\[([^\]]*)\]\(([^)]+)\)/g)) (m[1] ? images : links).push(m[3]);
    72	  for (const m of prose.matchAll(/<(https?:\/\/[^>]+)>/g)) links.push(m[1]);
    73	  for (const m of prose.matchAll(/\[(#\d+)\](?!:|\()/g)) links.push(defs.get(m[1]) ?? `MISSING-${m[1]}`);
    74	  return { headings, fences, code, cells, links, images };
    75	}
    76	
    77	function parseHtml(html: string): Parsed {
    78	  const main = html.slice(html.indexOf("<main>"), html.indexOf("</main>"));
    79	  const fences = [...main.matchAll(/<pre[^>]*><code>([\s\S]*?)<\/code><\/pre>/g)].map((m) => decode(m[1]));
    80	  const noPre = main.replace(/<pre[^>]*>[\s\S]*?<\/pre>/g, "");
    81	  const headings = [...noPre.matchAll(/<h[12][^>]*>([\s\S]*?)<\/h[12]>/g)].map((m) => norm(m[1].replace(/<[^>]+>/g, "")));
    82	  const code = [...noPre.matchAll(/<code>([\s\S]*?)<\/code>/g)].map((m) => decode(m[1]));
    83	  const cells = [...noPre.matchAll(/<t[dh]>([\s\S]*?)<\/t[dh]>/g)].map((m) => norm(m[1].replace(/<[^>]+>/g, "")));
    84	  const links = [...noPre.matchAll(/<a href="([^"]+)"/g)].map((m) => decode(m[1]));
    85	  const images = [...noPre.matchAll(/<img src="([^"]+)"/g)].map((m) => decode(m[1]));
    86	  return { headings, fences, code, cells, links, images };
    87	}
    88	
    89	const md = parseMd(MD);
    90	const html = parseHtml(HTML);
    91	
    92	// ---- 1. sections ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    93	
    94	describe("the status page", () => {
    95	  it("new page has the seven surface sections in order", () => {
    96	    const found = [...MD.matchAll(/^## (\d)\. (.*)$/gm)];
    97	    expect(found.map((m) => m[2])).toEqual(SURFACES);
    98	    expect(found.map((m) => Number(m[1]))).toEqual([1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7]);
    99	    const starts = found.map((m) => m.index ?? 0);
   100	    starts.forEach((start, k) => {
   101	      const body = MD.slice(start, k + 1 < starts.length ? starts[k + 1] : MD.length).split(/^## (?!\d\.)/m)[0];
   102	      let at = -1;
   103	      for (const label of LABELS) {
   104	        const idx = body.indexOf(`**${label}**`);
   105	        expect(idx, `${SURFACES[k]}: label ${label}`).toBeGreaterThan(at);
   106	        at = idx;
   107	      }
   108	    });
   109	    // The HTML twin has the same sections and labels.
   110	    const h = [...HTML.matchAll(/<h2 id="[^"]*">(\d)\. ([^<]*)<\/h2>/g)].map((m) => m[2]);
   111	    expect(h).toEqual(SURFACES);
   112	    for (const label of LABELS) expect(HTML.split(`<strong>${label}</strong>`).length - 1, label).toBe(7);
   113	  });
   114	
   115	  // ---- 2. twins --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
   116	
   117	  it("html twin matches md: headings, commands, table cells, link targets", () => {
   118	    expect(md.fences.length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
   119	    expect(html.fences.length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
   120	    expect(md.cells.length).toBeGreaterThan(20);
   121	    expect(html.cells.length).toBeGreaterThan(20);
   122	    expect(md.links.length).toBeGreaterThan(20);
   123	    expect(html.headings).toEqual(md.headings);
   124	    expect(html.fences.map(norm)).toEqual(md.fences.map(norm));
   125	    expect(html.code.map(norm)).toEqual(md.code.map(norm));
   126	    expect(html.cells).toEqual(md.cells);
   127	    // Link targets as a multiset (the md parser lists inline links, then autolinks, then [#N] references; the table of contents sits outside main).
   128	    expect([...html.links].sort()).toEqual([...md.links].sort());
   129	    expect(html.images).toEqual(md.images);
   130	    // Fragment links resolve to one id each in the same file.
   131	    const ids = [...HTML.matchAll(/\sid="([^"]+)"/g)].map((m) => m[1]);
   132	    expect(new Set(ids).size, "ids are unique").toBe(ids.length);
   133	    for (const m of HTML.matchAll(/href="#([^"]+)"/g)) expect(ids, `fragment #${m[1]}`).toContain(m[1]);
   134	  });
   135	
   136	  it("every command in a fenced block appears in the command table", () => {
   137	    const tableCells = md.cells.join("\n");
   138	    const lines = md.fences.flatMap((f) => f.split("\n")).map((l) => l.trim()).filter((l) => l && !l.startsWith("#"));
   139	    expect(lines.length).toBeGreaterThan(5);
   140	    for (const line of lines) expect(tableCells, line).toContain(line);
   141	  });
   142	
   143	  // ---- 3. links --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
   144	
   145	  it("every local link and screenshot in both twins resolves to a file", () => {
   146	    for (const [name, p] of [["md", md], ["html", html]] as const) {
   147	      expect(p.images.length, `${name} screenshots`).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(8);
   148	      for (const target of [...p.links, ...p.images]) {
   149	        if (/^(https?:|mailto:)/.test(target) || target.startsWith("#")) continue;
   150	        const file = resolve(DOCS, target.split("#")[0]);
   151	        expect(existsSync(file), `${name}: ${target}`).toBe(true);
   152	      }
   153	    }
   154	  });
   155	
   156	  // ---- 7. PR citations -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
   157	
   158	  it("every PR cited as #N appears in the link definitions", () => {
   159	    const defs = linkDefs(MD);
   160	    const body = MD.replace(/^\[#\d+\]: \S+$/gm, "");
   161	    const cited = new Set([...body.matchAll(/\[(#\d+)\](?!:|\()/g)].map((m) => m[1]));
   162	    expect(cited.size).toBeGreaterThan(20);
   163	    for (const c of cited) expect(defs.has(c), c).toBe(true);
   164	    for (const [k, url] of defs) expect(url, k).toBe(`https://github.com/treetopdevs/browser-life/pull/${k.slice(1)}`);
   165	    // Draft PRs are named as drafts where they are mentioned.
   166	    for (const n of ["#60", "#69", "#130"]) expect(MD, n).toMatch(new RegExp(`\\[${n}\\]`));
   167	    expect(MD).toMatch(/draft/i);
   168	  });
   169	});

   171	// ---- 4. screenshots ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
   172	
   173	interface ShotRow { file: string; route: string; viewport: string; width: number; crop: string; scale: string; row: string; errorPage?: boolean }
   174	
   175	function pngWidth(file: string): number {
   176	  const b = readFileSync(file);
   177	  expect(b.subarray(1, 4).toString("latin1"), `${file} is a PNG`).toBe("PNG");
   178	  return b.readUInt32BE(16);
   179	}
   180	
   181	describe("screenshots", () => {
   182	  const shotsDir = join(SLICE, "shots");
   183	  const manifest = JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(SLICE, "shots.json"), "utf8")) as ShotRow[];
   184	  const walkIds = new Set([...WALK.matchAll(/^\| (W\d+) \|/gm)].map((m) => m[1]));
   185	
   186	  it("png only, 8 to 16, each under 300 KB, header width 1280 or 375, each in shots.json and cited by the page", () => {
   187	    const files = readdirSync(shotsDir);
   188	    expect(files.filter((f) => !f.endsWith(".png")), "non-PNG files").toEqual([]);
   189	    expect(files.length).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(8);
   190	    expect(files.length).toBeLessThanOrEqual(16);
   191	    expect(manifest.map((m) => basename(m.file)).sort()).toEqual([...files].sort());
   192	    for (const f of files) {
   193	      const path = join(shotsDir, f);
   194	      expect(statSync(path).size, `${f} size`).toBeLessThan(300 * 1024);
   195	      const width = pngWidth(path);
   196	      expect([1280, 375], `${f} header width`).toContain(width);
   197	      const row = manifest.find((m) => basename(m.file) === f);
   198	      expect(row?.width, `${f} manifest width`).toBe(width);
   199	      expect(row?.viewport.startsWith(`${width}x`), `${f} viewport`).toBe(true);
   200	      expect(row?.crop && row.scale, `${f} crop and scale recorded`).toBeTruthy();
   201	      expect(walkIds.has(row?.row ?? ""), `${f} names an existing walkthrough row`).toBe(true);
   202	      expect(MD, `${f} cited by md`).toContain(`shots/${f}`);
   203	      expect(HTML, `${f} cited by html`).toContain(`shots/${f}`);
   204	    }
   205	  });
   206	});
   207	
   208	const basename = (p: string) => p.slice(p.lastIndexOf("/") + 1);
   209	
   210	// ---- 5. walkthrough verdicts ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
   211	
   212	interface WalkRow { id: string; route: string; verdict: string; assertion: string; evidence: string }
   213	
   214	function parseWalk(text: string): WalkRow[] {
   215	  const rows: WalkRow[] = [];
   216	  for (const line of text.split("\n")) {
   217	    if (!/^\| W\d+ \|/.test(line)) continue;
   218	    const c = line.trim().slice(1, -1).split(/(?<!\\)\|/).map((x) => x.trim().replace(/\\\|/g, "|"));
   219	    rows.push({ id: c[0], route: c[1].replace(/^`|`$/g, ""), verdict: c[5], assertion: c[6], evidence: c[7] });
   220	  }
   221	  return rows;
   222	}
   223	
   224	/** Problems with walkthrough rows: unknown verdict, empty assertion, evidence that does not exist, a works row on an error page. */
   225	export function walkProblems(rows: WalkRow[], exists: (rel: string) => boolean, errorPages: Set<string>): string[] {
   226	  const out: string[] = [];
   227	  for (const r of rows) {
   228	    if (!VERDICTS.includes(r.verdict)) out.push(`${r.id}: unknown verdict "${r.verdict}"`);
   229	    if (r.assertion.length < 15) out.push(`${r.id}: no deciding assertion`);
   230	    const refs = r.evidence.match(/(?:evidence|shots)\/[\w.-]+/g) ?? [];
   231	    if (refs.length === 0) out.push(`${r.id}: no evidence reference`);
   232	    for (const ref of refs) {
   233	      if (!exists(ref)) out.push(`${r.id}: evidence ${ref} does not exist`);
   234	      if (r.verdict === "works" && errorPages.has(ref)) out.push(`${r.id}: works, but ${ref} is flagged as an error page`);
   235	    }
   236	  }
   237	  return out;
   238	}
   239	
   240	describe("walkthrough", () => {
   241	  const rows = parseWalk(WALK);
   242	  const manifest = JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(SLICE, "shots.json"), "utf8")) as ShotRow[];
   243	  const errorPages = new Set(manifest.filter((m) => m.errorPage).map((m) => m.file));
   244	  const exists = (rel: string) => existsSync(join(SLICE, rel));
   245	
   246	  it("every surface and every inventory row has a verdict, an assertion and evidence that exists", () => {
   247	    expect(rows.length).toBeGreaterThan(40);
   248	    expect(walkProblems(rows, exists, errorPages)).toEqual([]);
   249	    // The page's own surface table.
   250	    const table = MD.slice(MD.indexOf("## Verdicts and screenshots"));
   251	    const surfaceRows = SURFACES.map((s) => table.split("\n").find((l) => l.startsWith(`| ${s} |`)));
   252	    surfaceRows.forEach((line, k) => {
   253	      expect(line, SURFACES[k]).toBeTruthy();
   254	      const c = (line as string).trim().slice(1, -1).split("|").map((x) => x.trim());
   255	      expect(VERDICTS, `${SURFACES[k]} verdict`).toContain(c[1]);
   256	      expect(c[2].length, `${SURFACES[k]} assertion`).toBeGreaterThan(15);
   257	      for (const id of c[4].match(/W\d+/g) ?? []) expect(rows.some((r) => r.id === id), `${SURFACES[k]} cites ${id}`).toBe(true);
   258	    });
   259	  });
   260	
   261	  it("negative cases: missing evidence, an unknown verdict and a works row on an error page fail", () => {
   262	    const base = { id: "W99", route: "/x", verdict: "works", assertion: "loads and its main action completes", evidence: "evidence/sweep.json" };
   263	    expect(walkProblems([base], exists, new Set())).toEqual([]);
   264	    expect(walkProblems([{ ...base, evidence: "evidence/nope.json" }], exists, new Set()).join()).toMatch(/does not exist/);
   265	    expect(walkProblems([{ ...base, evidence: "" }], exists, new Set()).join()).toMatch(/no evidence reference/);
   266	    expect(walkProblems([{ ...base, verdict: "fine" }], exists, new Set()).join()).toMatch(/unknown verdict/);
   267	    expect(walkProblems([{ ...base, assertion: "" }], exists, new Set()).join()).toMatch(/no deciding assertion/);
   268	    expect(walkProblems([{ ...base, evidence: "shots/01-site-home-1280.png" }], exists, new Set(["shots/01-site-home-1280.png"])).join()).toMatch(/error page/);
   269	    expect(walkProblems([{ ...base, verdict: "partly", evidence: "shots/01-site-home-1280.png" }], exists, new Set(["shots/01-site-home-1280.png"]))).toEqual([]);
   270	  });
   271	
   272	  it("the frozen inventory covers every Vite input and every playground destination", () => {
   273	    const vite = readFileSync(join(REPO, "apps/lab/vite.config.ts"), "utf8");
   274	    const inputs = [...vite.slice(vite.indexOf("input: {")).matchAll(/r\("\.\/([^"]+)"\)/g)].map((m) => m[1]);
   275	    expect(inputs.length).toBeGreaterThan(30);
   276	    const want = new Set<string>();
   277	    for (const f of inputs) {
   278	      if (f === "404.html") want.add("/no-such-page/");
   279	      else if (f === "index.html") want.add("/");
   280	      else if (f.endsWith("/index.html")) want.add(`/${f.slice(0, -"index.html".length)}`);
   281	      else want.add(`/${f}`);
   282	    }
   283	    const playground = readFileSync(join(REPO, "apps/lab/src/playground/page.ts"), "utf8");
   284	    for (const m of playground.matchAll(/href: "([^"]+)"/g)) want.add(m[1]);
   285	    expect(want.size).toBeGreaterThan(30);
   286	    const have = rows.map((r) => r.route.split(" ")[0].split("?")[0]);
   287	    for (const route of want) expect(have, `inventory row for ${route}`).toContain(route);
   288	  });
   289	});
   290	
   291	// ---- 6. the 18 October pages -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
   292	
   293	describe("18 October pages", () => {
   294	  // sha256 of the blobs at 0bdd7a70 (git show 0bdd7a70:docs/prototypes-2026-10-18.md | shasum -a 256, and the .html).
   295	  const PINNED: Record<string, string> = {
   296	    "prototypes-2026-10-18.md": "027cc25ab9b4e9620d8b5ab592337ee6f4a3091db30a31c87a10d5991f0230b3",
   297	    "prototypes-2026-10-18.html": "9b0b68705c4548360ca9c79a97544125b5ee0df85a2f8490298a605806760945",
   298	  };
   299	  const MARK = "prototypes-2026-11-02";
   300	
   301	  it("18 Oct pages unchanged but for one pointer line", () => {
   302	    for (const [name, sha] of Object.entries(PINNED)) {
   303	      const text = readFileSync(join(DOCS, name), "utf8");
   304	      const lines = text.split("\n");
   305	      const at = lines.flatMap((l, i) => (l.includes(MARK) ? [i] : []));
   306	      expect(at.length, `${name} has exactly one pointer line`).toBe(1);
   307	      // The line sits at the top: first line of the Markdown, the line right after <body> in the HTML.
   308	      if (name.endsWith(".md")) expect(at[0]).toBe(0);
   309	      else expect(lines[at[0] - 1]).toBe("<body>");
   310	      const rest = lines.filter((_l, i) => i !== at[0]).join("\n");
   311	      expect(createHash("sha256").update(rest).digest("hex"), name).toBe(sha);
   312	    }
   313	  });
   314	
   315	  it("the pinned hash test would catch a second change", () => {
   316	    const text = readFileSync(join(DOCS, "prototypes-2026-10-18.md"), "utf8").replace("Prototype status", "Prototype state");
   317	    const rest = text.split("\n").filter((l) => !l.includes(MARK)).join("\n");
   318	    expect(createHash("sha256").update(rest).digest("hex")).not.toBe(PINNED["prototypes-2026-10-18.md"]);
   319	  });
   320	});

# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## What this is

An open-ended artificial-life ecology on WebGPU: closed in matter, open in energy, exact integer physics. Individuals, reproduction and heredity are *inferred* from local rules, never declared (no `Organism` class, no `reproduce()`). Research goal and milestones (M3 bootstrap → M4/M5 ensembles): `docs/plan.md`. Rules in prose plus the arithmetic-bounds argument: `docs/rules.md`.

The repo is a pnpm workspace (TypeScript packages + Vite lab), a Deno toolchain for headless GPU runs, and a Phoenix app (`apps/coordinator`). It is jj-colocated with git (`.jj/`).

## Commands

```bash
pnpm install
pnpm dev                 # lab at http://localhost:5173 (/selftest.html, /island.html)
bin/dev                  # lab + coordinator together (PORT, LAB_PORT override 4000/5173)
pnpm typecheck           # tsc over packages/*/src, apps/lab/src, tests (excludes tests/deno)
pnpm test                # vitest: apps/lab/test, packages/*/test, experiments/test, tools/test
pnpm vitest run packages/sim-ref/test/ref.test.ts -t "<name>"   # single test
deno run -A tests/deno/gpu_golden.ts    # GPU vs CPU reference, bit for bit (native WebGPU)
npx playwright test      # browser golden (tests/e2e), Chrome with WebGPU flags; BL_ALL_ENGINES=1 adds firefox/webkit
BL_E2E_PORT=5241 npx playwright test   # a second worktree on its own port (default 5174; strictPort, never reuses a server)
cd apps/coordinator && mix test         # or `mix test test/coordinator/queue_test.exs`
cd apps/coordinator && mix precommit    # warnings-as-errors, format, test
pnpm gen:prereg          # regenerate experiments/preregistration.md generated sections
pnpm gen:cards           # redraw the link-preview cards (site + one per world) with Chrome/WebGPU; then look at them
```

Run `pnpm install` before any `deno run` in a fresh checkout (deno 2.9 otherwise writes `workspaces` into package.json). Tests that start deno pass `--no-lock` (`tools/test/deno-no-lock.test.ts` checks; a launcher or coordinator a test starts that runs deno itself gets the PATH shim in `tests/deno/lib/no_lock_deno.ts`), but a top-level `deno run`, or a tool that starts deno, still writes a stale deno.lock: when package.json deps change, update it deliberately (`deno cache --frozen tools/stitch.ts` fails while it is stale).

`tests/deno/*.ts` are standalone scripts (not `Deno.test`); run each with `deno run -A tests/deno/<name>.ts`. Several need a real GPU; `coordinator_integration.ts` spawns `mix phx.server` against a scratch data dir.

Experiment CLIs (`tools/`, all Deno, native WebGPU): `run.ts` (write run bundles to `runs/<experiment>/<preset>/<condition>/seed-<n>/`), `analyze.ts` (ensemble analysis), `bootstrap.ts` (M3 MAP-Elites search), `retest.ts`, `calibrate.ts`, `island.ts` (headless island), `stitch.ts` (coordinator segments → bundles). Usage is in each file's header comment and in README.md. `runs/` is gitignored and can hold very large outputs.

## Architecture

**Package imports.** `@bl/*` resolve to `packages/*/src/index.ts` via four separate maps that must stay in sync: `tsconfig.json` paths, `vitest.config.ts` aliases, `deno.json` imports, and `apps/lab/vite.config.ts` aliases. There is no build step for packages.

**Two implementations of one rule set.**
- `packages/sim-ref/src/step.ts` is the executable specification (CPU).
- `packages/sim-gpu/src/shaders.ts` (WGSL) must match it bit for bit. `packages/sim-gpu/src/golden.ts` defines golden cases and runs on any WebGPU host (Deno, browser `/selftest.html`, Playwright).
- `packages/sim-ref/test/golden-hashes.test.ts` pins state hashes per `RULE_VERSION` (in `packages/schema/src/config.ts`). Any change to dynamics changes the hashes: an intended rule change bumps `RULE_VERSION` and re-pins deliberately. A change that isn't meant to alter dynamics must leave every pin untouched — new optional `WorldConfig` keys stay absent from `defaultConfig()` so existing configs hash identically.
- All arithmetic is integer (u32 matter, fixed-point controllers, counter-based PRNG keyed on seed/step/cell, stochastic rounding with remainders to the source). Conservation residual is exactly zero. Keep new arithmetic inside the bounds listed in `docs/rules.md` (`MATTER_MAX`, `POOL_MAX`, `mulShr` limits); `validateConfig`/`validateState` enforce them.

**Packages.** `schema` (config, presets, layout, genome, worlds, checkpoint encoding, migration/exchange, M3 founder set) → `sim-ref`, `sim-gpu` → `metrics` (census, tracker, collectives, activity, complexity, stats; runs on readbacks) → `runner` (conditions, `runExperiment`, observers, stitching, island client) and `search` (MAP-Elites, gates). `lineage` (schema, sim-ref) is the lineage inspector's genotype core: keys, ancestry, mutation replay, the controller probe, and the mutation-edge store the lab keeps. `runner` code runs unchanged in browsers and Deno.

**Checkpoints and replay.** A checkpoint artifact carries physics state and observer state together, under one digest. Segmented runs must equal a continuous run byte for byte (`tests/deno/segments.ts`, `tests/deno/stitch.ts`); this determinism is what makes distributed verification possible.

**Archipelago.** `apps/coordinator` (Phoenix, JSON API under `/api`, static status page `priv/static/index.html`) hands islands segment tasks. `Coordinator.Queue` does scheduling, `Coordinator.Segment` owns segment state transitions, and `Coordinator.Store` is content-addressed storage for checkpoints (`.blck`) and bundle files. A fraction of segments is re-run by a second island and compared by state hash. Islands are `apps/lab/island.html` (browser) or `tools/island.ts` (Deno), both via `packages/runner/src/island.ts`. `apps/coordinator/AGENTS.md` has Phoenix/Elixir/OTP conventions to follow there; everything between its `usage-rules` markers is generated by `mix usage_rules.sync` (config in `mix.exs`), so rerun that after dependency changes rather than editing the section. `mix usage_rules.search_docs "<term>" -p <pkg>` searches hexdocs for the coordinator's deps.

**Lab.** `apps/lab/src/sim.worker.ts` owns the WebGPU device, frame loop, rendering and OPFS storage. `apps/lab/src/execution.ts` owns census cadence, observer integrity, checkpoint settlement and replay traversal. State-changing messages run through `exclusive()`, which waits for in-flight frame execution; generation identity invalidates stale readbacks.

**Pre-registration.** `experiments/endpoints.ts` is the source of truth for primary endpoints, held-out observables and activity thresholds, and `tools/analyze.ts` executes it. The generated sections of `experiments/preregistration.md` (between `GENERATED` markers) come from `pnpm gen:prereg`, and `experiments/test/prereg-sync.test.ts` fails when they are stale. Edit `endpoints.ts` and regenerate; never hand-edit inside the markers. The doc is frozen (2026-09-27): its SHA-256 is recorded in `experiments/FROZEN`, `pnpm gen:prereg` now refuses to rewrite the generated sections, and every change goes in a dated amendment section.

**Analysis scale.** Run bundles can be hundreds of MB per run (for example `lineages.tsv`). Analysis tools stream per census rather than loading whole tables; keep new analysis code streaming.

## Codex reviews

Reviews by Codex use **Sol 6.1 at High effort**, not Astra (standing instruction from 2026-10-03, replaces the earlier `gpt-6-astra` policy). Run them read-only at phase boundaries and always before committing:

```bash
codex exec -m gpt-6.1-sol -c model_reasoning_effort=high -s read-only -C <checkout> - < prompt.md > review.txt
```

Use `< /dev/null` when the prompt is an argument (otherwise codex waits on stdin) and `-o <file>` for the final message. Historical "Astra review" comments in the code are provenance for past reviews; leave them. Delegated agents and workspaces follow the same rule.

    33	moving-light, controller-locality, opponents, island.html, privacy), every site route, and all 15 destinations of the playground
    34	library (`apps/lab/src/playground/page.ts`: oases, seasons, bonds, motifs, boundaries, development, hungry shapes, matter, currents,
    35	spatial, ecosystem and its archive, vision screen, and the rest). `walkthrough.md` starts with this inventory as a table
    36	(route, section, action that must complete, evidence kind: shot / log / spec). Each row gets a verdict; a library page shot proves only
    37	that page. Pages owned by I03/I06 are visited as pages but their tools/ files are not touched. The 404 row's expected result is
    38	status 404 plus the site's not-found body (not a pass on 200).
    39	
    40	**Capture matrix.** 30+ routes at two widths exceeds the 16-shot cap, so shots are representative: one per umbrella section per
    41	width for the main action (about 14 files) plus the family-tree shot; every other inventory row is evidenced by an action log line
    42	(status, final DOM assertion text, console count) in `walkthrough.md`. The matrix is written into walkthrough.md before capturing.
    43	
    44	Browser checks use the playwright MCP at 1280x800 and 375x800. WebGPU needs Chrome flags: if the MCP browser lacks WebGPU,
    45	the lab shots are taken with the repo's Playwright Chrome (`BL_E2E_PORT=5311`, `channel: chrome`; native Deno runs `island.ts`/`run.ts` need real GPU regardless, record if absent) through a throwaway
    46	script in `/tmp`, and the page says which. Screenshots go to `docs/prototypes-2026-11-02/shots/NN-surface-WIDTH.png`:
    47	between 8 and 16, each PNG under 300 KB (downscale or crop with `sips`; jpeg not allowed). A filename suffix is not trusted: the test reads the PNG header, requires the real width to be 1280 or 375 (a crop or downscale is recorded in the table, with the browser viewport named separately), and a shots.json manifest lists file, route, viewport, crop/scale, and the walkthrough row it evidences.
    48	
    49	Verdict rules fixed before the walk: **works** = loads, no console error, the surface's main action visibly completes;
    50	**partly** = loads but a named action fails or needs an unlisted step; **broken** = does not load or crashes. A 200 alone is
    51	never "works" for a GPU page. `/selftest.html` works only if its expected-results list is non-empty, every case passed and `window.__golden.error` is unset (it renders failures as page text, so a clean console is not enough). A shot of an error page is evidence of broken, never works. Each verdict row also records the outcome-specific assertion that decided it. Console errors are copied into the evidence file verbatim.
    52	
    53	## 3. Fix policy
    54	
    55	Fix only if: at most about 3 files, no change to `packages/sim-ref`, `packages/sim-gpu` shaders, `RULE_VERSION`,
    56	golden pins or `defaultConfig()`, no `tools/` files that I03 (historical opponents) or I06 (niche construction) touch, no
    57	frozen preregistration GENERATED sections. Each fix gets a regression test where the code is testable (vitest or Playwright
    58	spec). Launchers: `bin/garden-dev` starts islands without `--no-lock`; run launchers with the PATH shim `tests/deno/lib/no_lock_deno.ts` provides (or confirm after every launcher run) and check `git status` shows no change to `package.json`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`, `deno.lock`; restore any stray change. Everything else goes in the page's "Follow-up slices" table: surface, exact repro commands, observed vs expected, suspected file.
    59

    78	
    79	`tools/test/prototypes-status.test.ts`:
    80	1. `new page has the seven surface sections in order` (headings Lab, Public site, Archipelago, Shared garden, AI naturalist, Evolution game, Research instruments, each followed by the four labels).
    81	2. `html twin matches md` : normalized text of both is compared (headings, commands from fenced blocks AND inline code and tables, verdict table cells, link targets, follow-up rows); extraction must be non-empty (zero fenced blocks fails); fragment links resolve to unique anchors in the same file.
    82	3. `every local link and screenshot in both twins resolves to a file`, and the screenshot set is non-empty.
    83	4. `screenshots: png only, 8 to 16, each under 300 KB, header width 1280 or 375, each in shots.json and cited by the page, each manifest row names an existing walkthrough row`.
    84	5. `every surface and every inventory row has a verdict of works, partly or broken with a nonempty assertion and an evidence reference that exists`; negative cases: a fixture with missing evidence, an unknown verdict, or a 'works' row whose evidence is flagged error-page fails validation.
    85	6. `18 Oct pages unchanged but for one pointer line`: pinned sha256 of the original md and html blobs (from 0bdd7a70) in the test; each edited file minus its single inserted pointer line must hash to the pinned value; no skip path.
    86	7. `every PR cited as #N appears in the link definitions` (formatting only). Provenance is separate and checked by hand with `gh pr view`: cited PRs are merged (#85 and #101 were closed unmerged, their work landed via #120 and #121, so cite those), and the draft PRs #60/#69/#130 are named as drafts.
    87	Each fix adds its own named case beside the code it fixes.

   124	is broken for a large reason is documented, not rebuilt. Status is reported on four separate axes per surface: implemented, locally verified (verdict), data available, deployed/externally verified. Live-host-only things (HTTPS, real phones) are 'not externally verified', not 'not built'.

    64	
    65	**What it is now.** The lab (`/lab/`) runs a world in the browser on WebGPU, with interventions (wound, feed, drain, sun),
    66	checkpoints and replay, and a Light and a Membrane view. Since 18 October: refused Feed and Drain no longer read as
    67	"World stopped" and the firsts log survives a reload ([#61]); the sun letter, a logged and replayable change to the light
    68	([#62]); a dedicated Membrane view ([#76]); Try receipts matched to the action asked ([#77]); the focused-view button kept
    69	inside the picker at 375 px ([#122]). The walk loaded the lab with no query ("Even light"), the breeder, gradient-m3 and a
    70	pond world, and ran `/selftest.html`, which passed 30 of 30 cases against the CPU reference on this Mac's GPU.

    74

    79	```

    89	  `/ecosystem/archive/` and `/vision-screen/`. `/soup/` ([#127]) is a public page linked from Worlds.

   122	## 3. Archipelago
   123	
   124	**What it is now.** The Phoenix coordinator (`apps/coordinator`) cuts runs into segments, hands them to islands (the
   125	browser's `/island.html` or the headless `tools/island.ts`), has a fraction replayed by a second island and compares state
   126	hashes. Since 18 October: islands release their leases and registration on a graceful stop ([#74]); operator counters show
   127	refusals and pruning ([#75]); the lease length is `BL_LEASE_MS` ([#78]); islands back off on explicitly unapplied requests
   128	([#83]); CI verifies the actual coordinator image ([#124]); production refuses to start without a discovery join token
   129	([#125]); a shared soup runs on the archipelago ([#127]). The walk created one tiny experiment, ran one segment on a Deno
   130	island, had a second island verify it (the segment reads "verified"), then ran a Chrome tab as an island through
   131	`/island.html`. The browser's segment 0 digest, `06e7413bbff0dd2a`, equals the native Deno island's for the same spec.
   132	Both islands shared one machine and one GPU type, so this is same-device-type verification.
   133	
   134	![Coordinator operations page at 1280 pixels](prototypes-2026-11-02/shots/07-coordinator-status-1280.png)
   135	![Coordinator operations page at 375 pixels](prototypes-2026-11-02/shots/07-coordinator-status-375.png)
   136	
   137	**How to try it.**
   138	
   139	```bash
   140	BL_DATA_DIR=/tmp/s1-coord-data LAB_PORT=5301 PORT=6301 bin/dev
   141	curl -sS -X POST -H 'content-type: application/json' http://127.0.0.1:6301/api/experiments -d '{"experiment":"s1-walk-1102","presetId":"spots","conditions":["treatment"],"seeds":[1],"steps":40,"segmentSteps":20,"censusEvery":10,"verifyFraction":1.0}'
   142	deno run -A --no-lock tools/island.ts --coordinator http://localhost:6301 --tasks 1
   143	```
   144	
   145	The curl answers `{"segments":2}`. Run the island line a second time to verify segment 0. Then open
   146	<http://localhost:6301/index.html> (the operations page) and <http://localhost:5301/island.html> (join from a tab; it
   147	does nothing until you press Join). `(cd apps/coordinator && mix phx.server)` runs the coordinator alone.
   148	
   149	**Not built.** Verification across different hardware (the walk had one GPU type). Distributed branch runs and hand or
   150	model pickers stay deferred ([archipelago breed](archipelago-breed-2026-10-18.md)). The coordinator's own test suite
   151	(`mix precommit`) was not rerun: no coordinator code changed.
   152	
   153	**Waits on you.** Deploy (item 1), the proxy fix (item 2), and setting the join token before any production start.

   155	## 4. Shared garden
   156	
   157	**What it is now.** One big storm world that volunteer islands grow a day at a time, with stones, the meteor ([#64]),
   158	sun letters with a founding switch ([#62], [#65]), prediction receipts and, since [#81], collective food on a local garden
   159	founded with `GARDEN_FOOD=1`. The backend is [#21]; the stone-horizon follow-up is [#59]. It is held private (the 24 October snapshot records that the release
   160	configuration keeps its files off the public site; this walk built no release image). The walk ran `bin/garden-dev` with food on: the garden was
   161	founded, days 0 to 4 were released, run by one island and replayed by the other (each day reads "verified"), a stone sent by
   162	curl landed in day 1, and a stone sent from the page was queued for day 16. Food has three separate states and they were
   163	recorded separately: **food enabled** yes (the public view says so); **request accepted** yes (a hand posted by curl was
   164	counted, 1 of 5); **collective actually fed** no, because one machine cannot make five networks. The page's own "Add my
   165	hand" was then refused by the per-network budget, which is a rule and not a fault.
   166	
   167	![Shared garden at 1280 pixels](prototypes-2026-11-02/shots/06-garden-1280.png)
   168	![Shared garden at 375 pixels](prototypes-2026-11-02/shots/06-garden-375.png)
   169	
   170	**How to try it.** Stop any other server on these ports first; the launcher starts its own lab, coordinator and two islands.
   171	
   172	```bash
   173	GARDEN_FOOD=1 LAB_PORT=5301 PORT=6301 GARDEN_DAWN_MS=5000 GARDEN_DATA_DIR=/tmp/s1-garden bin/garden-dev
   174	curl -sS -X POST -H 'content-type: application/json' -d '{"kind":"stone","x":128,"y":128,"r":8}' http://127.0.0.1:6301/api/public/gardens/commons/letters
   175	curl -sS -X POST -H 'content-type: application/json' -d '{}' http://127.0.0.1:6301/api/public/gardens/commons/food/hands
   176	```
   177	
   178	Then open <http://localhost:5301/garden/>. Food is read when the garden is founded, so `GARDEN_FOOD=1` must be on the
   179	first start. Stop it with Ctrl-C from a terminal; follow-up F2 records that a `SIGINT` sent from outside left the
   180	processes running.
   181	
   182	**Not built.** A real collective landing (needs five networks). Public opening: the release configuration keeps the
   183	garden private and scheduling off.
   184	
   185	**Waits on you.** Opening the commons (item 3), including the draft [#60].

   187	## 5. AI naturalist
   188	
   189	**What it is now.** A rule-based bell that rings on a departure from neutral runs, the lab's Field notes panel with a
   190	proposer's suggestion beside each note and a "Try it here" button only a person presses, and an offline notebook. Bell v2
   191	finished with a negative readout ([#66]); the prediction envelope for a naturalist that predicts interventions ([#135])
   192	and its fresh-family freeze ([#141]) are merged. The walk played gradient-m3 seed 1 to step 15,000 in 13 seconds: the panel
   193	went from "Listening" to rings with one note, "Fewer living cells: 21,452 at step 10,000, against a neutral median there of
   194	33,684", with its receipt. `tools/notebook.ts` ran on a neutral run with the full 40-run corpus present: all 40 envelope
   195	runs reproduced their record, and the neutral run rang 0 times, as it should.
   196	
   197	![Field notes at 1280 pixels, one note with its receipt and suggestion](prototypes-2026-11-02/shots/03-lab-gradient-m3-field-notes-1280.png)
   198	![Field notes at 375 pixels](prototypes-2026-11-02/shots/03-lab-gradient-m3-field-notes-375.png)
   199	
   200	**How to try it.** With the dev server from section 1:
   201	
   202	- <http://localhost:5301/lab/?world=gradient-m3&seed=1>, speed 9, Play until the step counter passes 15,000.
   203	- The offline notebook needs the neutral corpus under `runs/` (in the main checkout only; absent from a fresh clone):
   204	
   205	```bash
   206	deno run -A --no-lock tools/notebook.ts $RUNS/replay-calib/gradient-m3/neutral/seed-1001 --base $RUNS --out /tmp/s1-notebook --date 2026-11-02
   207	```
   208	
   209	where `RUNS` is `~/develop/browser-life/runs`. A committed example page is at
   210	[field-notebook-2026-10-17-gradient-m3-neutral-seed-15.html](examples/field-notebook-2026-10-17-gradient-m3-neutral-seed-15.html).
   211	
   212	**Not built.** The proposer bench has never run ([bench](naturalist-bench-v0-2026-10-18.md)). Notes stop at the first
   213	wound or feed, and a ring is not proof of emergence.
   214	
   215	**Waits on you.** The bench approval (item 5), paid calls (item 4) and the draft [#69] (item 6).
   216	
   217	## 6. Evolution game
   218	
   219	**What it is now.** Pond keeper on the breeder and the pond worlds: a log of firsts with rules stated in advance, a family
   220	tree, the pressure dial, Keep a lineage, weather and shells ([#71]), shell-ranked donors ([#73]) and a Membrane view
   221	([#76]). The walk bred nothing by hand; it stepped the breeder to t = 500 (16 starting families listed, 113 individuals),
   222	kept a lineage ("0:11"), and ran the pond cycle for eight seconds to t = 13,664 with nine firsts logged. The six pond specs
   223	(`lab-pond-keeper`, `-v1`, `-v2`, `-v3`, `lab-naturalist-actions`, `lab-naturalist`) passed 31 checks with 10 skipped by
   224	their own guards. Gene v2 and the polymer-world screens landed in [#71]; their readouts are Unclear or no pass at their
   225	stated scope, so they are results, not missing code.
   226	
   227	![The breeder at step 500 with its family tree, 1280 pixels](prototypes-2026-11-02/shots/02-lab-breeder-families-1280.png)
   228	![The breeder with its family tree, 375 pixels](prototypes-2026-11-02/shots/02-lab-breeder-families-375.png)
   229	![A pond cycle with the log of firsts, 1280 pixels](prototypes-2026-11-02/shots/04-lab-pond-firsts-1280.png)
   230	![A pond cycle with the log of firsts, 375 pixels](prototypes-2026-11-02/shots/04-lab-pond-firsts-375.png)
   231	
   232	**How to try it.** With the dev server from section 1: <http://localhost:5301/lab/?world=breeder&seed=1> (tick "Stop at each
   233	pond cycle", press +100 five times, read "Who's kin to whom?") and <http://localhost:5301/lab/?world=ponds-small&seed=1>
   234	(Play at speed 6 and watch the log of firsts). The repeatable checks:
   235	
   236	```bash
   237	BL_E2E_PORT=5311 npx playwright test lab-pond-keeper.spec.ts lab-pond-keeper-v1.spec.ts lab-pond-keeper-v2.spec.ts lab-pond-keeper-v3.spec.ts lab-naturalist-actions.spec.ts lab-naturalist.spec.ts lab-worlds.spec.ts lab-lineage.spec.ts public-routes.spec.ts public-not-found.spec.ts public-site.spec.ts --project=chrome --workers=1
   238	```
   239	
   240	**Not built.** A family split needs 5,000 steps of censuses, so the short walk saw none; the vitest tests carry that rule.
   241	Hail and new breeding scores are deferred ideas. Human playtests have not run.
   242	
   243	**Waits on you.** The playtest and the next gene direction (item 7).
   244	
   245	## 7. Research instruments
   246	
   247	**What it is now.** Headless run, lineage and report tools on native WebGPU, plus the I-series pages and results landed on
   248	2 November. The walk wrote a 3,000-step run bundle with `tools/run.ts` (conservation exact, 37 lineages), built a lineage
   249	dossier for its top lineage with `tools/lineage.ts` (1 of 1 ancestors verified against the recorded genomes), and rendered
   250	it as an HTML report with `tools/report-html.ts`. Merged since 18 October: the recovery assay evidence ([#72]), the creative
   251	playground and food-band experiments ([#120], [#121]; [#85] and [#101] were closed unmerged and their work landed through
   252	those), the byte-exact program soup port ([#126], [#128]), the I01 atlas slices 1 and 2 ([#132]), the predictability atlas
   253	and observer benchmark ([#133]), the controller-locality matrix ([#136], [#145]), the historical-opponent assay first rung
   254	([#139]), the builder to receiver transfer first rung ([#142]), the moving-light navigation benchmark ([#144]) and the
   255	assembly-inspired observer calibration ([#148]). Their local report pages (`/predictability/`, `/assembly/`, `/niche/`,
   256	`/moving-light/`, `/controller-locality/`, `/opponents/`) all loaded with no console error; they are static reports and no
   257	spec covers them.
   258	
   259	![Lineage dossier report at 1280 pixels](prototypes-2026-11-02/shots/08-research-lineage-report-1280.png)
   260	![Lineage dossier report at 375 pixels](prototypes-2026-11-02/shots/08-research-lineage-report-375.png)
   261	
   262	**How to try it.**
   263	
   264	```bash
   265	deno run -A --no-lock tools/run.ts --experiment s1-walk --preset spots --conditions treatment --seeds 1 --steps 3000 --census 100 --lineage-obs --out /tmp/s1-runs
   266	deno run -A --no-lock tools/lineage.ts --dir /tmp/s1-runs/s1-walk/spots/treatment/seed-1 --rule top --out /tmp/s1/dossier.json
   267	deno run -A --no-lock tools/report-html.ts lineage /tmp/s1/dossier.json --out /tmp/s1/lineage-report.html --status PILOT
   268	```
   269	
   270	Never run `tools/run.ts` bare: its defaults are 20,000 steps and the checkout's `runs/`. Open the written HTML in a browser.
   271	
   272	**Not built.** The I03 historical-opponent tournament and the I06 comparators belong to parallel slices and are not
   273	described here. The large scientific corpora live in `runs/` of the main checkout and are absent from a fresh clone.
   274	
   275	**Waits on you.** The draft [#130] (item 6), AWS and paid calls (item 4).
   276

    68	## Capture matrix
    69	
    70	Representative shots, one per umbrella section per width, 16 files in `shots/`, all native 1280 or 375 pixels wide by 800 tall (browser viewport equals the image: no crop, no scale). Each PNG was passed through `pngquant` (lossy palette, 55-85 quality) to stay under 300 KB. Every other inventory row is evidenced by its log line in `evidence/sweep.json` (status, h1, canvas count, console errors, horizontal scroll, external requests) and by the Playwright spec named in its row. `shots.json` lists file, route, viewport, crop, scale and the walkthrough row.

     4	supersedes [the 18 October status](prototypes-2026-10-18.md) and the [24 October snapshot](prototypes-2026-10-24.md). It

     7	surface below was run on this machine the way a person would, at 1280 and 375 pixels, and given a verdict. The raw record

    42	what `bin/dev` and `bin/garden-dev` ask for when `apps/coordinator/deps` is missing. Commands below are the
    43	port-qualified forms that were run here (lab on 5301, coordinator on 6301) so that parallel work never collided. The
    44	default-port forms (5173 and 4000) are in the command table at the end, marked as read, not run. Use `pnpm dev --port N`
    45	without a `--` separator: pnpm would pass the separator on and Vite would ignore the flags.

    63	## 1. Lab
    64	
    65	**What it is now.** The lab (`/lab/`) runs a world in the browser on WebGPU, with interventions (wound, feed, drain, sun),
    66	checkpoints and replay, and a Light and a Membrane view. Since 18 October: refused Feed and Drain no longer read as
    67	"World stopped" and the firsts log survives a reload ([#61]); the sun letter, a logged and replayable change to the light
    68	([#62]); a dedicated Membrane view ([#76]); Try receipts matched to the action asked ([#77]); the focused-view button kept
    69	inside the picker at 375 px ([#122]). The walk loaded the lab with no query ("Even light"), the breeder, gradient-m3 and a
    70	pond world, and ran `/selftest.html`, which passed 30 of 30 cases against the CPU reference on this Mac's GPU.
    71	
    72	![Selftest at 1280 pixels: 30 of 30 cases pass](prototypes-2026-11-02/shots/05-selftest-1280.png)
    73	![Selftest at 375 pixels](prototypes-2026-11-02/shots/05-selftest-375.png)
    74	
    75	**How to try it.**
    76	
    77	```bash
    78	pnpm dev --port 5301 --strictPort
    79	```
    80	
    81	- <http://localhost:5301/lab/>: the default world.
    82	- <http://localhost:5301/lab/?world=breeder&seed=1>: the breeder (see section 6).
    83	- <http://localhost:5301/lab/?world=gradient-m3&seed=1>: Field notes (see section 5).
    84	- <http://localhost:5301/selftest.html>: the CPU reference against this browser's GPU, bit for bit, in about 20 seconds.
    85	- <http://localhost:5301/worlds/>: every world and what it asks; each card links into the lab.
    86	- The prototype library at <http://localhost:5301/playground/> lists 15 pages ([#120] landed them): `/particles/`,
    87	  `/particles/picker/`, `/particles/oases/`, `/particles/seasons/`, `/particles/bonds/`, `/particles/motifs/`,
    88	  `/boundaries/`, `/hungry-shapes/`, `/development/`, `/matter/`, `/currents/`, `/spatial/`, `/ecosystem/`,
    89	  `/ecosystem/archive/` and `/vision-screen/`. `/soup/` ([#127]) is a public page linked from Worlds.
    90	
    91	**Not built.** Five library pages (`/playground/`, `/development/`, `/hungry-shapes/`, `/ecosystem/`,
    92	`/ecosystem/archive/`) have no icon link, so a first load asks for `/favicon.ico` and logs a 404: their verdict is
    93	**partly**, follow-up F1. Hail, a shell-respecting Wound and more weather controls are deferred in
    94	[pond keeper v3](pond-keeper-v3-2026-10-22.md). The creative pages are noted in `docs/creative-prototypes/README.md` as
    95	kept out of the production site image until separately approved.
    96	
    97	**Waits on you.** Deploy (item 1). Approving the library pages for release.

    99	## 2. Public site
   100	
   101	**What it is now.** Home, Worlds, How it works, About, Participate, Privacy, Status, the research journal and the 404 page,
   102	plus `/soup/` ([#127]). The journal was brought current twice ([#67], [#123]). Missing-page analytics and sitemap dates were
   103	closed in [#80]; CI builds and checks the actual site image ([#89]). The walk fetched all eight pages and the 404 at both
   104	widths: every h1 present, no horizontal scroll, no external request, and the `public-routes`, `public-site`,
   105	`public-not-found` and `public-a11y` browser specs passed. An unknown path answers 404 with the site's own page.
   106	
   107	![Public home at 1280 pixels](prototypes-2026-11-02/shots/01-site-home-1280.png)
   108	![Public home at 375 pixels](prototypes-2026-11-02/shots/01-site-home-375.png)
   109	
   110	**How to try it.**
   111	
   112	- <http://localhost:5301/>, `/research/`, `/worlds/`, `/how-it-works/`, `/about/`, `/participate/`, `/privacy/`, `/status/`
   113	  and a missing path such as `/no-such-page/`, with the dev server from section 1.
   114	- Under bare `pnpm dev` the dev proxy has no coordinator: `/status/` and `/garden/` log a 502 and show their unavailable
   115	  state. Start `bin/dev` (section 3) to see them with data.
   116	
   117	**Not built.** The build check inside `public-routes` is skipped unless `apps/lab/dist` exists; no `pnpm build` was run.
   118	Live-domain behaviour, real devices and screen readers are not externally verified.
   119	
   120	**Waits on you.** Deploy (item 1) and the proxy fix (item 2).
   121	
   122	## 3. Archipelago
   123	
   124	**What it is now.** The Phoenix coordinator (`apps/coordinator`) cuts runs into segments, hands them to islands (the
   125	browser's `/island.html` or the headless `tools/island.ts`), has a fraction replayed by a second island and compares state
   126	hashes. Since 18 October: islands release their leases and registration on a graceful stop ([#74]); operator counters show
   127	refusals and pruning ([#75]); the lease length is `BL_LEASE_MS` ([#78]); islands back off on explicitly unapplied requests
   128	([#83]); CI verifies the actual coordinator image ([#124]); production refuses to start without a discovery join token
   129	([#125]); a shared soup runs on the archipelago ([#127]). The walk created one tiny experiment, ran one segment on a Deno
   130	island, had a second island verify it (the segment reads "verified"), then ran a Chrome tab as an island through
   131	`/island.html`. The browser's segment 0 digest, `06e7413bbff0dd2a`, equals the native Deno island's for the same spec.
   132	Both islands shared one machine and one GPU type, so this is same-device-type verification.
   133	
   134	![Coordinator operations page at 1280 pixels](prototypes-2026-11-02/shots/07-coordinator-status-1280.png)
   135	![Coordinator operations page at 375 pixels](prototypes-2026-11-02/shots/07-coordinator-status-375.png)
   136	
   137	**How to try it.**
   138	
   139	```bash
   140	BL_DATA_DIR=/tmp/s1-coord-data LAB_PORT=5301 PORT=6301 bin/dev
   141	curl -sS -X POST -H 'content-type: application/json' http://127.0.0.1:6301/api/experiments -d '{"experiment":"s1-walk-1102","presetId":"spots","conditions":["treatment"],"seeds":[1],"steps":40,"segmentSteps":20,"censusEvery":10,"verifyFraction":1.0}'
   142	deno run -A --no-lock tools/island.ts --coordinator http://localhost:6301 --tasks 1
   143	```
   144	
   145	The curl answers `{"segments":2}`. Run the island line a second time to verify segment 0. Then open
   146	<http://localhost:6301/index.html> (the operations page) and <http://localhost:5301/island.html> (join from a tab; it
   147	does nothing until you press Join). `(cd apps/coordinator && mix phx.server)` runs the coordinator alone.
   148	
   149	**Not built.** Verification across different hardware (the walk had one GPU type). Distributed branch runs and hand or
   150	model pickers stay deferred ([archipelago breed](archipelago-breed-2026-10-18.md)). The coordinator's own test suite
   151	(`mix precommit`) was not rerun: no coordinator code changed.
   152	
   153	**Waits on you.** Deploy (item 1), the proxy fix (item 2), and setting the join token before any production start.
   154	
   155	## 4. Shared garden
   156	
   157	**What it is now.** One big storm world that volunteer islands grow a day at a time, with stones, the meteor ([#64]),
   158	sun letters with a founding switch ([#62], [#65]), prediction receipts and, since [#81], collective food on a local garden
   159	founded with `GARDEN_FOOD=1`. The backend is [#21]; the stone-horizon follow-up is [#59]. It is held private (the 24 October snapshot records that the release
   160	configuration keeps its files off the public site; this walk built no release image). The walk ran `bin/garden-dev` with food on: the garden was
   161	founded, days 0 to 4 were released, run by one island and replayed by the other (each day reads "verified"), a stone sent by
   162	curl landed in day 1, and a stone sent from the page was queued for day 16. Food has three separate states and they were
   163	recorded separately: **food enabled** yes (the public view says so); **request accepted** yes (a hand posted by curl was
   164	counted, 1 of 5); **collective actually fed** no, because one machine cannot make five networks. The page's own "Add my
   165	hand" was then refused by the per-network budget, which is a rule and not a fault.
   166	
   167	![Shared garden at 1280 pixels](prototypes-2026-11-02/shots/06-garden-1280.png)
   168	![Shared garden at 375 pixels](prototypes-2026-11-02/shots/06-garden-375.png)
   169	
   170	**How to try it.** Stop any other server on these ports first; the launcher starts its own lab, coordinator and two islands.
   171	
   172	```bash
   173	GARDEN_FOOD=1 LAB_PORT=5301 PORT=6301 GARDEN_DAWN_MS=5000 GARDEN_DATA_DIR=/tmp/s1-garden bin/garden-dev
   174	curl -sS -X POST -H 'content-type: application/json' -d '{"kind":"stone","x":128,"y":128,"r":8}' http://127.0.0.1:6301/api/public/gardens/commons/letters
   175	curl -sS -X POST -H 'content-type: application/json' -d '{}' http://127.0.0.1:6301/api/public/gardens/commons/food/hands
   176	```
   177	
   178	Then open <http://localhost:5301/garden/>. Food is read when the garden is founded, so `GARDEN_FOOD=1` must be on the
   179	first start. Stop it with Ctrl-C from a terminal; follow-up F2 records that a `SIGINT` sent from outside left the
   180	processes running.
   181	
   182	**Not built.** A real collective landing (needs five networks). Public opening: the release configuration keeps the
   183	garden private and scheduling off.
   184	
   185	**Waits on you.** Opening the commons (item 3), including the draft [#60].
   186	
     1	// The 2 November 2026 prototype status (docs/prototypes-2026-11-02.md and its HTML twin) and the walk-through it was written
     2	// from (docs/prototypes-2026-11-02/). CPU only: reads files, starts nothing. What is checked: the seven surface sections in
     3	// order with their four labels; the twins carry the same headings, commands, table cells and link targets; every local link and
     4	// screenshot resolves; the screenshot set (PNG only, 8 to 16, each under 300 KB, header width 1280 or 375, listed in
     5	// shots.json and cited by both twins); every inventory row of the walk has a verdict, a deciding assertion and evidence that
     6	// exists, and the frozen inventory covers every Vite input and playground destination; the 18 October pages differ from
     7	// their original blobs by one pointer line only; every PR cited as [#N] has a link definition.
     8	import { createHash } from "node:crypto";
     9	import { existsSync, readdirSync, readFileSync, statSync } from "node:fs";
    10	import { join, resolve } from "node:path";
    11	import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
    12	import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
    13	
    14	const REPO = fileURLToPath(new URL("../..", import.meta.url));
    15	const DOCS = join(REPO, "docs");
    16	const SLICE = join(DOCS, "prototypes-2026-11-02");
    17	const MD = readFileSync(join(DOCS, "prototypes-2026-11-02.md"), "utf8");
    18	const HTML = readFileSync(join(DOCS, "prototypes-2026-11-02.html"), "utf8");
    19	const WALK = readFileSync(join(SLICE, "walkthrough.md"), "utf8");
    20	
    21	const SURFACES = ["Lab", "Public site", "Archipelago", "Shared garden", "AI naturalist", "Evolution game", "Research instruments"];
    22	const LABELS = ["What it is now.", "How to try it.", "Not built.", "Waits on you."];
    23	const VERDICTS = ["works", "partly", "broken"];
    24	
    25	// ---- parsing helpers --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    26	
    27	const decode = (s: string) => s.replace(/&lt;/g, "<").replace(/&gt;/g, ">").replace(/&quot;/g, '"').replace(/&#x27;|&#39;/g, "'").replace(/&amp;/g, "&");
    28	const norm = (s: string) => decode(s).replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim();
    29	
    30	/** The md source with fenced blocks cut out (returned separately). */
    31	function splitFences(md: string): { prose: string; fences: string[] } {
    32	  const fences: string[] = [];
    33	  const prose = md.replace(/^```[^\n]*\n([\s\S]*?)^```\s*$/gm, (_m, body: string) => { fences.push(body.replace(/\n$/, "")); return ""; });
    34	  return { prose, fences };
    35	}
    36	
    37	function linkDefs(md: string): Map<string, string> {
    38	  const defs = new Map<string, string>();
    39	  for (const m of md.matchAll(/^\[(#\d+)\]: (\S+)$/gm)) defs.set(m[1], m[2]);
    40	  return defs;
    41	}
    42	
    43	/** Plain text of a markdown fragment: markup removed so it compares with the HTML's text. */
    44	function mdText(s: string): string {
    45	  return norm(
    46	    s
    47	      .replace(/!\[([^\]]*)\]\(([^)]+)\)/g, "")
    48	      .replace(/\[([^\]]+)\]\(([^)]+)\)/g, "$1")
    49	      .replace(/<(https?:\/\/[^>]+)>/g, "$1")
    50	      .replace(/\\\|/g, "|")
    51	      .replace(/`/g, "")
    52	      .replace(/\*\*/g, ""),
    53	  );
    54	}
    55	
    56	interface Parsed { headings: string[]; fences: string[]; code: string[]; cells: string[]; links: string[]; images: string[] }
    57	
    58	function parseMd(md: string): Parsed {
    59	  const defs = linkDefs(md);
    60	  const { prose: withDefs, fences } = splitFences(md);
    61	  const prose = withDefs.replace(/^\[#\d+\]: \S+$/gm, "");
    62	  const headings = [...prose.matchAll(/^#{1,2} (.*)$/gm)].map((m) => mdText(m[1]));
    63	  const code = [...prose.matchAll(/`([^`]+)`/g)].map((m) => m[1].replace(/\\\|/g, "|"));
    64	  const cells: string[] = [];
    65	  for (const line of prose.split("\n")) {
    66	    if (!line.startsWith("|") || /^\|[\s|:-]+\|$/.test(line)) continue;
    67	    for (const c of line.trim().slice(1, -1).split(/(?<!\\)\|/)) cells.push(mdText(c));
    68	  }
    69	  const links: string[] = [];
    70	  const images: string[] = [];
    71	  for (const m of prose.matchAll(/(!?)\[([^\]]*)\]\(([^)]+)\)/g)) (m[1] ? images : links).push(m[3]);
    72	  for (const m of prose.matchAll(/<(https?:\/\/[^>]+)>/g)) links.push(m[1]);
    73	  for (const m of prose.matchAll(/\[(#\d+)\](?!:|\()/g)) links.push(defs.get(m[1]) ?? `MISSING-${m[1]}`);
    74	  return { headings, fences, code, cells, links, images };
    75	}
    76	
    77	function parseHtml(html: string): Parsed {
    78	  const main = html.slice(html.indexOf("<main>"), html.indexOf("</main>"));
    79	  const fences = [...main.matchAll(/<pre[^>]*><code>([\s\S]*?)<\/code><\/pre>/g)].map((m) => decode(m[1]));
    80	  const noPre = main.replace(/<pre[^>]*>[\s\S]*?<\/pre>/g, "");
    81	  const headings = [...noPre.matchAll(/<h[12][^>]*>([\s\S]*?)<\/h[12]>/g)].map((m) => norm(m[1].replace(/<[^>]+>/g, "")));
    82	  const code = [...noPre.matchAll(/<code>([\s\S]*?)<\/code>/g)].map((m) => decode(m[1]));
    83	  const cells = [...noPre.matchAll(/<t[dh]>([\s\S]*?)<\/t[dh]>/g)].map((m) => norm(m[1].replace(/<[^>]+>/g, "")));
    84	  const links = [...noPre.matchAll(/<a href="([^"]+)"/g)].map((m) => decode(m[1]));
    85	  const images = [...noPre.matchAll(/<img src="([^"]+)"/g)].map((m) => decode(m[1]));
    86	  return { headings, fences, code, cells, links, images };
    87	}
    88	
    89	const md = parseMd(MD);
    90	const html = parseHtml(HTML);
    91

    96	    const found = [...MD.matchAll(/^## (\d)\. (.*)$/gm)];
    97	    expect(found.map((m) => m[2])).toEqual(SURFACES);
    98	    expect(found.map((m) => Number(m[1]))).toEqual([1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7]);
    99	    const starts = found.map((m) => m.index ?? 0);
   100	    starts.forEach((start, k) => {

    25	        <p class="eyebrow">A different chemistry · life from noise</p>
    26	        <h1>Programs that learn to copy themselves.</h1>
    27	        <p class="lede">Every square on the grid is a 64-byte program, random at the start. Each step, neighbours pair up: their bytes are joined into one tape, run as a tiny self-editing program, and split back. Nothing rewards copying and nothing declares a birth. Sooner or later a program that happens to copy itself into its partner appears, and its copies spread across the grid.</p>
    28	        <p class="soup-honest"><b>The wait is long and depends on luck.</b> On the paper’s 240 × 135 grid, one of our two seeds grew copiers, after about 17,000 steps; the other did not in the time we ran it. To skip the wait, click a square and press <b>Plant copiers here</b>.</p>

   116	      <section class="shell prose-grid soup-explain">
   117	        <div class="aside-label">How to read it</div>
   118	        <div class="prose">
   119	          <h2>Colour marks copies.</h2>
   120	          <p>In the family view a random soup is dark: almost every program is one of a kind. When copiers take over, a coloured patch grows outward from where they started. Each family has its own colour, picked at random from its instructions. A mutation that changes an instruction makes a new family with an unrelated colour, so competing variants look speckled, and a variant that wins turns its region one colour.</p>
   121	          <p>The byte view draws what the paper draws: every program as an 8 × 8 block of its bytes. It shows the most on a large screen, where copies of one program appear as a shared texture.</p>
   122	          <p>Each program only ever meets programs within two squares of it, so families spread as fronts instead of mixing at once. Mutation keeps throwing in random bytes; copiers survive it by copying faster than it wears them down.</p>
   123	        </div>
   124	      </section>
   125	      <section class="shell prose-grid soup-explain">
   126	        <div class="aside-label">Where it comes from</div>
   127	        <div class="prose">
   128	          <h2>A port of a published experiment.</h2>
   129	          <p>This is Google’s cubff, the code behind <cite>Computational Life</cite> (Agüera y Arcas and colleagues, 2024), ported to run in your browser and checked byte for byte against the original. It is not the lab’s chemistry: there is no matter, light or energy here, only programs rewriting each other. We keep it beside the jars because it asks the same question from the other end: what does it take for copying to start?</p>
   130	          <p>Without WebGPU the page falls back to a smaller 120 × 68 grid on the processor, which is much slower.</p>
   131	        </div>

   782	/** Header controls reached in the order they are drawn: along a row left to right, rows top to bottom. */
   783	function orderProblems(stops: Focused[]): string[] {
   784	  const head = stops.filter((s) => s.inHeader);
   785	  const out: string[] = [];
   786	  for (let k = 1; k < head.length; k++) {
   787	    const [a, b] = [head[k - 1].box, head[k].box];
   788	    const sameRow = Math.abs((a.y + a.height / 2) - (b.y + b.height / 2)) < Math.max(a.height, b.height) / 2;
   789	    if (sameRow ? b.x <= a.x : b.y <= a.y) out.push(`${head[k].desc} comes after ${head[k - 1].desc} but is drawn before it`);
   790	  }
   791	  return out;
   792	}

   794	const SKIP: Record<string, { link: string; target: string; header: string } | null> = {
   795	  "/lab/": { link: ".skip-link", target: "#lab-main", header: "header.top" },
   796	  [POND]: { link: ".skip-link", target: "#lab-main", header: "header.top" },

   798	  "/soup/": { link: ".skip", target: "#soup-main", header: ".site-header" },
   799	  // One short page whose `<main>` is the first thing in it: nothing to skip.

   801	};
   802	const skipFor = (route: string) => (route in SKIP ? SKIP[route] : { link: ".skip", target: "#main", header: ".site-header" });
